## 1. Purpose

The user asked for something "beautiful", "stylish" and "full of stuff". T11 is where that claim is tested, and where the separately built layers become one look. Seven parallel owners will not produce that on their own (§12).

After T11, these are observably true on the integrated build:

1. **All 36 palette and biome combinations hold up.** A contact sheet of real zones shows every tile with a clear sun, a framed road, layered objects and a disciplined palette. No tile is grey, flooded, empty or broken.
2. **Wash-out cannot pass unnoticed.** Five numeric gates hold on 180 frames. Two of them are §6.7's near-white and mean chroma. The other three fix the weakness T06 reported: those two pass a visibly flooded frame.
3. **Zone changes feel like travel.** Palette crossfades never turn the frame beige for several seconds, which a straight OKLab mix did on several palette pairs in rehearsal (section 10). No pop, no hard edge.
4. **The first ten seconds impress.** A cold load reaches a lit, moving, populated frame quickly, with no white flash, and opens on a deliberately chosen zone rather than whatever seed 1985 hashes to.
5. **It stays clean in motion and inside budget.** Motion evidence with objects on shows no flicker, and §9.2's proxy gate holds in all six biomes.
6. **The operator has watched it live and given a verdict.** That verdict, not the numbers, closes §14 risk 2.

Items 1 to 5 can be established as facts about pixels on one RTX 4070 SUPER. **Nothing an agent does here establishes beauty.** Section 2 says exactly what the review can and cannot show. T11 stays `blocked` until item 6, and is not `done` without it.

## 2. Context and orientation

Working directory for every command: `C:\highsierralabs\terrain`. It is not a git repository. `§n` means a section of `architecture.md`. T11 starts only when T00 to T10 are `done` and every suite is green, because T11 is the only writer while it runs (§12). Port 5111, output folders below. Read §5.4, §6, §8.1, §11.5, §14 and `Agents.md` first.

Terms:

- **Window:** T11's temporary ownership: `src/render/**`, `src/world/palettes.ts`, `src/world/biomes.ts`, `src/contracts/glsl/hero.glsl`, `src/feel/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/tuning.ts`. It closes when this plan is `done`.
- **Pair:** one palette and biome combination, indexed by `Biome` and `PaletteId` (§8.1).
- **Sample:** one state `(chunk, local, tick)` in a real zone. **Tile:** the fixed sample chosen to represent a pair (M1).
- **Round:** one capture, review and tuning cycle, numbered `r00` upward. Output is in `test-results/T11-art/<round>/`.
- **Gate:** a numeric check in `tests/art/support/thresholds.ts`. **Rubric:** the human-readable scoring table in section 5.
- **Drill:** a small deliberate break in a window file, proving a gate can fail on the real path, then restored by hash.
- **Planted sheet:** a contact sheet with hidden defects, used to test the reviewer before the reviewer's scores count.
- **Centre haze:** mean Rec.709 luma inside uv radius .26 of `min`, minus the same frame under `forceFeel({ bloomGain: 0 })`, in code values.
- **Floor black:** 25th percentile of luma in the floor band, rows 612 to 1079.
- **Lit saturation:** mean of `(max - min) / max` over pixels with `max(r, g, b) >= 32`.
- **Slit scan:** one row or column of pixels stacked over time, so motion becomes an image. **Heat map:** per-pixel temporal change over a sequence, amplified.
- **Pin:** a test in another task's files that asserts a value T11 may legally retune. T11 edits it, under the pin rule (§12).
- **Ledger:** the list of every value T11 changed in another owner's file. **Deviation register:** the same, for values that now differ from `architecture.md`.

**Where the evidence comes from.** Every `[O]` here ran on 2026-11-05 in a throwaway scratchpad with the pinned `three`, `postprocessing` and Chromium 1234 on the RTX. None of it is project code and none counts as acceptance. Limits that matter:

- The art prototype renders Sunset Drive only. Its `placeChunks` moves terrain and **not its instanced objects** [O: line 101 of its `index.html`; slit scans show vertical object lines], so its temporal numbers describe the floor only. That is §14 risk 4 exactly.
- T03's rehearsal renders terrain and sky through DevPost with no props, CA, Crt or vignette.

### What this review can and cannot establish

The reviewer is an agent looking at images. That is a real instrument with known limits.

**It can establish:**

- Numeric facts about captured frames on this GPU: the gates.
- Gross defects in stills. In a blind rehearsal on a 6 x 6 sheet of 320 x 180 tiles, with the key hidden and the answer written first, the planner flagged 9 of 10 planted defects. Bloom excess at three strengths, an 8% lift, a dim frame and a missing object layer were clear. 40% desaturation and a 15% tint were flagged as probable. A subtle gain 1.5 was flagged only as uncertain. 20% desaturation was missed, and there was one false alarm [O]. **This is an optimistic figure.** The tiles were hue-rotated copies of one scene, so defects stood out against near-identical neighbours, and the same agent wrote the script and knew the defect pool. One run, one reviewer.
- Relative comparisons: this tile against its neighbours, this round against the last.
- Fine texture at 1:1. Images near 1920 px wide show unscaled: 1 px gratings, a 3 px scan pattern at 10% and grain of plus or minus 4 levels were all distinguishable at 1920 x 1080, and 8 px text was readable [O].
- Temporal proxies: numbers, slit scans, heat maps and frame strips. A naive grid against the filtered grid was visible in a slit scan of the floor [O].

**It cannot establish:**

- **That it is beautiful, stylish or impressive to a person.** A rubric score is one opinion in a fixed format. Another agent agreeing is correlated evidence, not independent confirmation, because it is the same kind of model reading the same images.
- **Motion as perceived:** speed, flow, judder, frame pacing, strobing, nausea. Stills and proxies miss these by construction.
- **Colour on a real display:** wide gamut, HDR, panel brightness, room light. The pipeline is 8-bit sRGB. Steps of one code level are not visible to the reviewer [O: a three-step ramp looked flat], which is why banding is numeric plus range-stretched crops.
- **A defect that hits every tile equally.** The rehearsal's detection came from outliers. A uniform drift has none, so the reference tile, round deltas and gates must catch it.
- **Sound, sync, game feel or fun.**
- **Anything on another GPU or a phone.**

A larger sheet is downscaled for viewing and its gratings alias into false moire [O: 3840 x 1620 displayed at 2000 x 844, so the limit is probably 2000 px on the long edge, inferred from that one notice]. **Every image meant for a reviewer stays at or under 1920 px wide.**

**Left for the human operator:** the verdict on beauty and style, the first ten seconds seen cold, motion and speed, colour on a real panel, whether transitions feel like travel, audio with picture, the screensaver test, and a real mid-range device if one is available (§14 risks 1 and 2). M10 gives a pack and a fixed question list. The operator's answer overrides any agent score, in either direction.

## 4. Interfaces

**Consumed from `src/contracts/index.ts`:** `HarnessApi` members `ready`, `manual`, `warp`, `renderAt`, `stepFrames`, `setPost`, `setMsaa`, `setLayerVisible`, `forceZone`, `forceBend`, `forceFeel`, `stats`, `pixels`; `HarnessStats`; `ZoneState`; `Tier`, `PostVariant`, `HeroMode`, `LayerName`, `FeelState`; `Biome`, `PaletteId`, `PALETTE_NAMES`, `BIOME_NAMES`; `CHUNK_LEN`, `ZONE_CHUNKS`, `DEFAULT_SEED`.

**Consumed in Node, from tests only:** `createWorld` from `src/world/index.ts`, `findZoneChunk` from `src/world/zones.ts` (T01 offers this for T11). T00's lint table places no restriction on `tests/art/**`. `washout` from `src/render/fx/dev/metrics.ts` for the conformance test.

**Consumed from T00:** `test`, `expect`, `expectRasterizer`, `saveShot` from `tests/support/pw.ts`; projects `gpu`, `gpu-timing`, `software`; `npm run test:art`, `check`, `check:task -- T11`, `plans:check`, `contracts:check`.

**Consumed from T10:** the URL switches `?harness`, `?period=0`, `?tier=N`, `?seed=<int>`. The one `console.info('MV_READY')` after `createHarness` returns. The read-only `window.__mvE2e` hook, which T10 offers to T11: `host.ctx.world` and `boot`. `startScreencast` and `flipbook.html` from `tests/e2e/lib/view.ts`, imported read-only, so T11 does not duplicate them.

**Consumed from T08:** DOM hooks `data-mv="title"`, `launch`, `hud`, and `<html data-mv-mode>`.

**Consumed from T05:** the `sheet:` tests of `tests/visual/props.spec.ts` for the silhouette sheet and `maxPairIoU`.

**Consumed from T09:** `npm run test:forever` and `npm run test:perf`, including its shimmer metric and proxy gate. T11 does not redefine shimmer.

**Provided:** nothing to production code. The suites, and these helper shapes:

```ts
// tests/art/support/capture.ts
export interface Sample { chunk: number; local: number; tick: number; palette: number; biome: number; into: number }
export async function openHarness(page: Page, info: TestInfo, o?: { seed?: number; tier?: Tier; period?: number }): Promise<void>;
export async function goto(page: Page, s: Sample): Promise<void>;           // manual(true) already on; warp, then assert stats().chunk
export async function frame(page: Page, s: Sample, o?: { post?: PostVariant | 'off'; layers?: Partial<Record<LayerName, boolean>>; feel?: Partial<FeelState> }): Promise<{ w: number; h: number; rgba: Uint8Array }>;
export function tickFor(chunk: number, local: number): number;              // Math.round(((chunk % 320) * 64 + local) * 1.5)

// tests/art/support/metrics.ts, all pure, rgba top-down
export function nearWhitePct(w: number, h: number, rgba: Uint8Array): number;   // min(r,g,b) > 235
export function meanChroma(w: number, h: number, rgba: Uint8Array): number;     // mean(max - min)
export function centreHaze(w: number, h: number, on: Uint8Array, off: Uint8Array, maskR?: number): number;
export function floorBlack(w: number, h: number, rgba: Uint8Array, firstRow?: number): number;
export function litSaturation(w: number, h: number, rgba: Uint8Array): number;
export function clippedPct(w: number, h: number, rgba: Uint8Array): number;     // max(r,g,b) >= 254
export function changesPerLevel(w: number, h: number, rgba: Uint8Array, box: Box): number[];  // T04's banding metric
export function discOcclusion(w: number, h: number, all: Uint8Array, skyOnly: Uint8Array, cam: { fovDeg: number; pitch: number }): number;
export function propCoverage(w: number, h: number, on: Uint8Array, off: Uint8Array, lastRow: number): number;
export function emptyColumns(w: number, h: number, on: Uint8Array, off: Uint8Array, lastRow: number): number;
```

`tickFor` keeps cruise timing: 120 ticks per 80 u is 1.5 ticks per u, and 320 chunks is 30720 ticks, the slowest phase period (§4.5).

**Seams.**

| Other task | Topic | T11's assumption |
|---|---|---|
| T10, T09 | Page URL | `/?harness&period=0&tier=3&seed=<int>` gives `window.__mv` over an **aperiodic** world at tier 3, per T10's URL table. §5.1's default `periodChunks = 1024` holds only zones 0 to 10, where most of the 36 pairs do not exist. `capture.spec` proves it or fails. `openHarness` waits for the `MV_READY` console line, then `__mv.ready` |
| T01, T09 | `forceZone` is not a biome switch | `overrideZone` changes palette, hero and the sky's biome key only. Terrain and props follow the chunk's real biome (T01). So the 36 pairs come from real zones, and `forceZone` is used only for hero by palette, at one fixed zone |
| T09 | `pixels()` | Returns the final composited canvas in RGBA, `canvas.width * canvas.height * 4` bytes, valid only in the same task as the render. Rows are bottom-up (§8.1). `capture.ts` flips them and keeps its screenshot comparison as a check |
| T09, T10 | `renderAt` | Through T10's `renderState`: step 10 runs if `warpSerial` changed, neutral feel, alpha 0, no sim step, `camX` and `camY` from the sim's last position, so the ship and camera hold still. A jump over 64 u, or the camera moved backwards at all, rebuilds. A forward step within 64 u streams as in play. **`renderState` does not move the sim,** so T11 asserts `stats().chunk` and `.local` only right after a `warp`, never after a `renderAt` |
| T03, T05, T10 | Jumps | T05 counts a crossing only when `v.cam.chunk` rises by exactly 1, and T10's jump rule rebuilds otherwise. T11 calls `warp` before a non-adjacent `renderAt` anyway, so sequences start from a clean state |
| T09, T10 | Tier | `?tier=N` sets `CreateGovernor.forced`, with no boot timing (T10). One page load per tier. `stats().tier` is asserted, because this RTX would otherwise boot at tier 4 and gates must read tier 3, the default. `HarnessApi.setTier` serves switches within a page (C-33) |
| T09 | Shimmer baseline | T09 stores a per-machine baseline. A T11 retune legitimately moves it. T11 re-stores it only at M9, with a Decision log entry and reviewer agreement |
| T09 | Proxy gate biome | §9.2 says "heaviest biome". T11's `budget.timing` measures all six, so a retune that changes which is heaviest cannot hide |
| T10 | Default seed | T10 and its `realWorld.test.ts` read `DEFAULT_SEED`, so the opening seed stays tested (C-35) |
| T10 | Page's own zone | `window.__mvE2e.host.ctx.world.zoneAt(at, out)` under `?harness` gives the zone the page really rendered. T11 builds `out` in the page. T10 offers this hook to T11 and it is not a contract, so `capture.spec` fails loudly if it is absent. CCR-T11-2 is withdrawn for this reason |
| T10 | Poster | `index.html`'s poster is Sunset Drive's colours and is hidden with no fade on the first real frame, with the disc in the same place. A palette retune or a non-Sunset opening does not reach it, and a mismatch reopens T10. T11 reports it. This favours a Sunset Drive opening (M7) |
| T10 | Taste list | T10's M7 sends "a question of taste" to T11. T11 reads T10's Progress and Outcomes at M0 and takes those into its first findings |
| T10 | `dist/` | `npm run build` gives a page that works from a nested path, so `first10.spec` serves `dist/` with `node:http` under `/art/`, as T00's build proof does |
| T06, T09 | Metric definitions | Near-white is `min(r,g,b) > 235`, mean chroma is mean `max - min`, both on final 8-bit pixels (§8.1). T05's own variants are not used. `metrics.spec` compares T11's result with T06's `washout` on one image. T11's centre haze is not §8.1's whole-frame haze lift: same reference, inside uv radius .26 |

T11 assumes that comparison reads the table in `palettes.ts`, with the literal pin only on Sunset Drive's horizon, which T11 does not change. `mixPalette` keeps its signature, stays allocation-free and pure, and returns the endpoints within 1e-6 at m = 0 and 1 |

| T02 | Feel | Neutral under `renderAt`: `bloomGain` 1, `speedLines` 0. Real feel under `stepFrames`. Feel caps are in the window |

| T00 | `test:art` and `check:task` | `test:art` runs `tests/art` under `gpu`, and config routes `*.timing.spec.ts` to `gpu-timing` only. `check:task -- T11` prints `vitest: not applicable`, so it does not run other tasks' tests. T11 therefore uses whole-repo `npm run check` at each milestone |
| T00 | Playwright output | `--output` is wiped per run, so round artifacts live in the sibling `test-results/T11-art/`, which is not an output folder |

## 5. Plan of work

Each milestone can be verified alone. T11 runs alone, so whole-repo checks are always legal.

### M0. Pre-flight

**Goal:** know what was actually shipped. **Work:** confirm T00 to T10 are `done`. Run every suite once to get a clean baseline. Read `plans/T09-harness.md`, `plans/T10-integrate.md` with its Progress and Outcomes, and `src/contracts/index.ts`. Correct the seam table above against disk. Take backup `r00` per section 6. **Result:** go, or `blocked` with the reason. **Proof:** A0.

### M1. Instruments

**Goal:** tools that report the truth, built before anything is judged. **Work:**

**Samples.** For a pair, `z0 = findZoneChunk(seed, p, b)`, the first chunk of its first zone. Samples sit at `into` 8, 23, 38, 53, 68, with `local` 20 and `tick = tickFor(chunk, local)`. If a RING lies 0 to 8 chunks ahead, meaning `chunk % 48` is 0 or at least 40, add 9 chunks, or subtract 9 if adding would pass `into` 74. **The tile is always the `into` 38 sample, after that rule.** This is fixed in advance, so nobody chooses flattering frames.

**Captures per sample,** at 1920 x 1080, DSF 1, tier 3, MSAA 2:

| Render | Used by |
|---|---|
| `full`, all layers | W1, W2, W4, W5, C1, tiles |
| `min` twice, default and with bloom gain 0, all layers | W3 |
| `bare`, all layers | D1, D2 and O1, the frame with everything |
| `bare`, props hidden | D1, D2 |
| `bare`, sky only | O1 |
| `min`, sky only | B1 (added at M2, Decision log) |

That is 1,260 frames with the two added at execution (Decision log); this paragraph's first figure, 900, counted five. Rehearsed readback is about 6 ms per frame in the page, and about 200 ms when also transferred to Node and encoded [O], so about 3 minutes. Use `test.setTimeout(900_000)`, since T00's default is 60 s. Temporal specs compute in the page.

**The numeric gates.** All live in `thresholds.ts`. Evidence is on the art prototype unless noted.

| Id | Gate | Limit | Why this limit |
|---|---|---|---|
| W1 | Near-white, `full` | at most .5% | §6.7. Prototype .003% |
| W2 | Mean chroma, `full` | at least 55 | §6.7. Prototype 71 |
| W3 | Centre haze, uv radius .26 | at most 18 [A] | Table below |
| W4 | Floor black | at most 10 [A] | Table below |
| W5 | Lit saturation | at least .70 [A] | Table below |
| C1 | Clipped: `max >= 254`, `full` | at most 1% [A] | §11.5's histogram. Clean .16%, gain 3 .55%, flood 2.18% [O] |
| B1 | Banding: T04's changes per level, sky crop x 64 to 320, rows 0 to 240, on `min`, each channel spanning 8 levels or more | at least 1.3 | T04's A10. `setPost('off')` is the control, T04 measured 1.00. Stars and nebula can only inflate this on a real frame, so it is weaker than T04's isolated run [I]. Range-stretched crops are viewed too |
| O1 | Disc occlusion, non-canyon | at most 15% | §11.5, §14 risk 12. Disc pixels above the horizon, by the analytic §8.2 test, where `bare` all-layers differs from `bare` sky-only by over 24 on any channel. Canyon is printed and viewed |
| D1 | Prop coverage above the horizon: pixels changed over 12 by props | at least 8% each sample, and each biome's median at least .4 of the median across biomes [A] | "Full of stuff" as a floor, not a target. Prototype 39.2%, with large pyramids [O], so calibrate at M2 |
| D2 | Empty screen columns: 16 columns above the horizon, centre 4 excluded, under .5% coverage | at most 1 of 12 [A] | Mirrors T01's screen column rule on real pixels |

| X1 | Real frame at m = .5 (`into` 87, `local` 32), all 30 ordered pairs | chroma at least 50, lit saturation at least .60, W1 [A] | OKLab 27.1 and .494 on Sunset to Toxic; OKLCh 56.3 and .731 [O, stand-in stack] |
| X2 | Pop: 1500 frames at 1.3333 u across a transition, largest full-frame mean luma step | at most 1.0 code value, and p99.9 of 64 x 36 block steps at most 1.5 times the same biome's plain cruise [A] | Relative, since no moving-object baseline exists. Drill DR5 must fail it |

Objects were static in the prototype, so the object band is uncalibrated until M2 |
| S2 | Motion is present: near floor mean frame diff | at least 2 [A] | Prototype 5.4. Stops S1 passing on a frozen frame |
| S3 | Shimmer | T09's gate | §11.3, with objects on. T11 adds nothing |
| F1 | First lit frame after navigation, `dist/` | at most 3 s [A], and no black frame afterwards | Cold load |
| F2 | White flash: 64 x 36 mean luma of any screencast frame | at most 140 [A] | The poster and the first frames must not blow out |
| P1 | Proxy gate per biome, tier 3 | p95 at most 2.0 ms, calls at most 68, triangles at most 1.5M | §9.2 |
| P2 | Tier 0 on SwiftShader | at most 50 ms | §9.2 |

**The wash-out fix.** T06 showed near-white and mean chroma pass a flood. On the real prototype scene, across three positions, that holds, and the three additions separate what those two miss [O]:

| Variant | Near-white % | Chroma | Centre haze | Floor black | Lit sat | Read when viewed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Bloom off | .001 | 69 | 0 | 3 | .90 | |
| **Clean,** threshold .95, intensity .8 | .003 | 71 | 9.0 | 3 | .88 | Crisp |
| Gain 1.25 (T02's cap) | .003 | 71 | 11.1 | 3 | .87 | |
| Gain 1.5 (T06's clamp) | .004 | 71 | 13.0 | 3 to 4 | .86 | |
| Gain 2 | .004 | 71 | 15.9 | 3 to 4 | .84 | |
| Gain 3 | .001 | 70 | **21.0** | 3 to 4 | .81 | Thick glow, visible contour rings in the halo |
| Threshold .1, intensity .8 | .002 | 73 | **21.1** | 6 to 10 | .83 | |
| Threshold .5, intensity 2 | .003 | 68 | **32.0** | 4 to 5 | .77 | Pale sun, washed centre |
| Flood, threshold .1, intensity 3 | .007 | 74 | **58.7** | **11 to 19** | .70 | Plainly flooded, yet chroma rises |
| Lift 8% toward white | .003 | 65 | n/a | **23** | **.66** | Grey sky |
| Lift 15% toward white | .004 | 60 | n/a | **40** | **.48** | |
| 15% toward mid purple | 0 | 68 | n/a | **14** | .73 | |
| Desaturate 20% | .003 | 57 | n/a | 3 | .80 | Mild, passes all |
| Desaturate 40% | .003 | **42** | n/a | 3 | **.68** | |
| Dim to 60% | 0 | **42** | n/a | 1 | .86 | Dark, not washed |

Bold fails a limit. Every flood passes W1 and W2. Centre haze catches bloom excess, floor black catches lifted blacks from any cause, and lit saturation catches pale colour from any cause. Together they fail everything visibly wrong in the table, and they pass the feel caps T02 and T06 allow. Desaturation of 20% passes all five, and that is left to viewing.

Why a centre disc: with the same variant as reference the vignette cancels, so the mask is not needed for validity. It stays because the difference is more sensitive there: clean to flood is 9.0 to 58.7, against 6.4 to 35 over the whole frame [O]. It varied by .1 across positions [O]. That is worth keeping under its own name, because two gates with one name and two meanings would mislead a reader. This also matches how the table was measured, against a bloom-off render of the same stack. It was not measured through T06's variants, which M2 must confirm.

Limits are [A] because only Sunset Drive has been measured. M2 sets them on real frames. **As set at M2 and frozen at M3, the limits and the definitions that changed are in Progress (M2's calibration table) and the Decision log; `tests/art/support/thresholds.ts` holds them, each with its source.** The table above is the plan as approved.

**Truth checks in `capture.spec`:** row order matches a screenshot. Buffer is 1920 x 1080. All alpha 255. `stats().tier` is 3. `stats().chunk` equals the request straight after `warp`. **Each pair's label comes from the page's own zone,** through `window.__mvE2e.host.ctx.world.zoneAt`, and equals the request, so a mislabelled sheet is impossible. Frames at `c` and `c + 1024` differ, which proves the world is aperiodic.

**Sheets.** All at or under 1920 px wide. Tiles are box-filtered from `pixels()` by an integer factor. Labels are HTML text in a strip above each tile, 12 px, rendered in a second blank page with `setContent` and data URIs, then screenshotted. `sheet.ts` also writes the HTML, for the operator.

| File | Layout | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `sheet36.png` | 6 x 6, 320 x 180. Rows palette, columns biome | Whole-set read, colour rhythm |
| `pal-0.png` to `pal-5.png` | 3 x 2, 640 x 360 | **Tile scoring.** One palette, six biomes |
| `biome-0.png` to `biome-5.png` | 3 x 2, 640 x 360 | One biome, six palettes |
| `biome-blind.png`, `biome-blind-key.json` | 6 x 3, 320 x 180, unlabelled, shuffled | Can a reader name the biome? |
| `hero-palette.png` | 6 x 4, 320 x 180 | §5.4 hero modes on the real scene, through `forceZone` at one fixed zone |
| `ring-palette.png` | 3 x 2, 640 x 360 | RING at 465 u, on the disc outline |
| `bend.png` | 3 x 2, 640 x 360 | Six biomes at `forceBend(8e-5, -1.2e-5)`, the §5.3 extreme: verticals hold, the road still leads to the disc |
| `worst-<gate>-<n>.png` | 1920 x 1080 | Three worst frames per gate, chosen by number. Read these, not the flattering ones |
| `crop-*.png` | 960 x 400, 1:1 | Horizon, far objects, floor, stretched sky |
| `ab-<pair>.png` | Before and after | Same state, this round and `r00` |

**Deliverable:** `npm run test:art` runs. Nothing in `src/` has changed. **Proof:** A1 to A3.

### M2. Round 0: baseline, calibration, reviewer test

**Goal:** see the integrated build honestly, before touching it. **Work:**

1. Run everything with `TERRAIN_ROUND=r00`.
2. **Calibrate limits, once.** For each [A] limit, record the distribution over all samples. A limit must sit between the worst frame the reviewer accepts and the mildest drill or plant that should fail. If those overlap on real frames, the metric does not separate here: say so, make it report-only, and rely on viewing. That does not apply to a flood detector or a density detector, see M3. Log each change. **After M3, limits are frozen,** and a change needs a Decision log entry and the reviewer's agreement (`Agents.md`).
3. **Test the reviewer.** Generate the planted sheet from the real build (`plant.ts`): 6 to 10 tiles, positions and count from `crypto`, key written and never printed. Pool: `flood`, `noprops` (real `setLayerVisible`), `lift.08`, `dim.6`, `desat.4` are **strong**; `desat.2`, `toward.15`, `glow` are **weak**. Open the sheet, write `plant-answer.txt`, then open the key. **All strong plants must be found, with at most 3 false alarms.** Report weak results as a sensitivity figure. A failure means the image path or reader is unreliable: look at why, retry once with a new sheet, then stop (U7).
4. Score the full rubric. Write the findings list, most severe first, each with its evidence file.

**Expected at `r00`, stated beforehand so a surprise is visible:** X0 passes, since T01's test requires it. X1 sits near its limit on Sunset to Toxic and Toxic to Ember [O: 56.3 and 55.8, no props]. The mid-slopes of default terrain look bare [O: T01]. The opening is Ultraviolet at deepest night [O: section 10].

**Result:** `r00` complete, limits set, reviewer sensitivity known. **Proof:** A4, A5.

### M3. Drills

**Goal:** prove the gates can fail on the real path, not only on synthetic images. **Work:** one at a time: back up, make the smallest possible change, run the named spec with `TERRAIN_ROUND=drill-DRn`, record, restore, compare hashes. Do not tune anything during this milestone.

| Id | Deliberate break | Spec | Must fail | Prior evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DR1 | Bloom threshold .1 and base intensity 3, the constants in `src/render/fx/post.ts` and `postParams.ts`, with the gain clamp lifted | `sheet36` | W3 and W4, **while W1 and W2 still pass** | Prototype 58.7, 11 to 19, .007%, 74 |
| DR2 | Terrain grid lines replaced by a hard step, in `src/render/terrain/terrain.frag.glsl` | `motion` | S1 far or mid floor | Naive 3.44 and 16.4 against .92 and 4.5 |
| DR3 | `KEEPOUT_K = 1` in `biomes.ts` | `sheet36` | O1 in Neon City | T01 CPU projection 24.3% |
| DR4 | Quotas times .25 for one biome in `biomes.ts` | `sheet36` | D1 or D2 for that biome | T01's floors fail too, as expected |
| DR5 | Palette mix stepped at m = .5 in `palettes.ts` | `transition` | X2 | Untested |
| DR6 | Neon edge `L` tripled in `src/render/props/**` | `sheet36` | W3 or C1 | Untested |
| DR7 | Straight OKLab in `mixPalette`, in `palettes.ts` | `transition` | X0 and X1 on Sunset to Toxic | 27.1 chroma, .18 saturation |
| DR8 | Arctic Laser disc top back to `#FFFFFF` in `palettes.ts` | `sheet36` | W1 on Arctic Laser tiles | T04 1.46%, T06 .97% |

A drill that does not fail means that gate proves nothing on this scene. Fix the metric or demote it to report-only, with a Decision log entry. Do not proceed with a vacuous gate. If W3 does not fail DR1, try §8.1's whole-frame haze lift at §6.7's limit. If neither fails, set `blocked` (U13), because R2 then has no flood detector. Likewise DR4: if neither D1 nor D2 fails it after fixing the metric, set `blocked` (U14), because "full of stuff" (R3) then has no detector on real pixels, only T01's CPU floors and a viewer's score.

DR7 and DR8 are the fail-before for X0, X1 and W1 on Arctic Laser.

**Result:** each gate has a real-path failure on record. **Proof:** A6.

### The rubric

Used by every reviewer, on every round, in this exact form. **Scores are ordinal reading aids.** They force specific looking and allow comparing rounds by the same reviewer. They are not measurements, and they are never averaged, so strong glow cannot buy back an empty biome. Each score carries one line of evidence naming what was seen. "Looks good" is not evidence.

| # | Criterion | Scope, source | 0: reject | 1: weak | 2: good | 3: strong |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| J1 | **Composition** | Each tile, `pal-*` | No focal point, disc hidden, or horizon off row 590 by over 20 px | Disc visible, but one side empty or clutter crowds its lower third | Disc dominant, V-notch reads, both sides framed | As 2, plus three depth planes (road furniture, mid scenery, far skyline) and a leading line into the vanishing point |
| J2 | **Silhouette variety** | Each tile, `biome-*` | One shape type, or every object a box | Two types, or all one size | Three or four nameable types, two size classes | Five or more, skyline rhythm, biome nameable with the label covered |
| J3 | **Density** | Each tile plus D1, D2 | A third of the width empty in the mid band, or a road side bare | Objects only near, or only far | Near and mid filled, gaps read as deliberate | Near, mid and far layered, no bare stretch in any of the 5 samples, road still readable |
| J4 | **Colour discipline** | Each tile, `sheet36` | Grey or muddy, or a hue foreign to the palette dominates | Palette reads, with a dull area or an overused accent | Dark bodies, two or three dominant hues, accent sparing | As 2, and instantly distinct from the other five palettes, with every archetype readable in it |
| J5 | **Glow** | Each tile, `crop-*`, `worst-W3` | Floor cells or sky flooded, disc flat white, contour rings in the halo | Haze around busy areas, or thick lines | Thin lines glow, gaps stay dark, disc keeps gradient and bands | As 2, and glow follows importance: shards and gates brightest, far edges faint |
| J6 | **Hero disc** | `hero-palette`, `ring-palette` | Position or size wrong, or mode unreadable | Reads, but muddy or low contrast | Mode reads at thumbnail size | Real presence in every mode, and its floor reflection reads |
| J7 | **Cohesion and grounding** | Each tile, `crop-*` | Floating or buried objects, z-fighting, mirror glitches | One minor defect, or one archetype out of key | Everything sits on the ground and shares the neon treatment | As 2, and fog dissolves objects into the sky with no dark smudge over the disc |
| J8 | **Zone transition** | Transition strips, curves | Grey-out, pop or hard edge | Continuous, dull middle | Continuous, midpoint acceptable, arrival of the new biome visible | Midpoint attractive in itself. New objects emerge from fog before the palette completes. Reads as travel |
| J9 | **First ten seconds** | `first10.png`, screencast frames | Blank over 3 s, white flash, empty frame, or title hides the disc | Attractive but sparse or static: sun and grid only | At 1 s: sun and three or more object types. Title clean. Change visible along the strip | As 2, plus an event inside the window (gate, saucer, searchlight, ring on approach), on a strong palette |
| J10 | **Motion evidence** | Slit scans, heat maps, strips, S1 to S3 | Hot spots in the far band, flicker, crawl, pop | Mild crawl in one biome | No defect visible in any proxy | **Agents may not award 3.** Operator only |
| J11 | **Synthwave identity** | `sheet36` at thumbnail size | Does not read as 80s synthwave | Neon, but generic | Reads as 80s synthwave | **Agents may not award 3.** Operator only |

**Agent-stage pass:** no 0 anywhere. On J1 to J5 and J7, at least 2 on at least 30 of 36 tiles, and every 1 has a note. J6, J8, J9, J10, J11 at least 2. All gates green. A gate and a viewer that disagree are a finding to investigate, never settled by quietly preferring one.

**Viewing order, every round, to limit anchoring:** `sheet36.png`, five lines of first impression, written down. `pal-*` and tile scores. Strips and motion evidence. **Then** `gates.txt` and `delta.txt`. Then `worst-*`. Rubric tables go in `test-results/T11-art/<round>/rubric.md`, with a summary line in Progress.

**Blind naming, independent reviewer only:** biome from `biome-blind.png`, at least 14 of 18, with each biome at least 2 of 3 [A; chance is 3 of 18]. Objects from T05's unlabelled silhouette sheet, at least 25 of 27 [A]. T05 itself said an independent review would be stronger than its own.

### Round protocol

One round, from M4 onward:

1. Back up the window (section 6).
2. Choose **one theme,** from the current milestone's list. Write the hypothesis: which criterion or gate, which knob, and what should change.
3. Make the change. Add a ledger row: file, name, old, new, round, reason.
4. `npm run check:task -- T11`, then `npx vitest run tests/unit/world` if a world file changed.
5. Run the specs for that theme, or all of `test:art` if a global value changed.
6. Review in the viewing order. Open `ab-*.png` for the pairs the change targeted **and** for two pairs it did not.
7. **Keep or revert.** Keep only if the target improved and no other gate or score regressed, per `delta.txt`. Otherwise restore the backup and log why. A revert is a good outcome.

**Stopping.** At most 12 rounds in total before M10 and 3 per theme [A]. Two rounds in a row without improvement end that theme. If a criterion stays at 0 at the cap, that is U6. A 1 at the cap goes to the operator as a named choice.

Tune in this order, because later steps sit on earlier ones: photometry, palette, terrain and sky balance, density, individual archetypes, motion, transitions, opening.

**Knobs.** Fix the source of a problem before compensating downstream.

| Symptom | First knob | File | Re-run, and what it may flip |
|---|---|---|---|
| Beige midpoint, X0, X1 | Per-slot hue or chroma | `palettes.ts` | `tests/unit/world`, `transition` |
| W3 high | Lower the offending source's `L` first (§6.7), then bloom `radius`, then `intensity`. Keep `luminanceThreshold` below the disc's 1.05 (T06) | Owning layer, then `src/render/fx/post.ts`, `postParams.ts` | `test:art`; T06's A5 pin on intensity. T06's A15 stays on its card |
| Contour rings in halo | Bloom `intensity` or `radius` down | `src/render/fx/post.ts`, `postParams.ts` | `crop-*` viewed; T06's A5 pin |
| W4 high | Surface base terms `top * .16`, `mid * .20`, then mirror strength | `src/render/terrain/terrain.frag.glsl` | T03 visual |
| Grid weak or heavy | `L` 1.45, weights .55 and .75 | `terrain.frag.glsl` | Shimmer, S1 |
| Sky fights disc | Nebula `L`, stars, then the disc's halo weights in `hero.glsl` | `src/render/sky/skyParams.ts`, `src/contracts/glsl/hero.glsl` | B1, O1 |
| Far objects appear as smudges, or terrain ends too soon | `FOG_START` and `FOG_END`, lower only | Section 12, T00 | T03's A9, T09's edge paint |
| Biome thin | Quotas, then bands, within `perChunk` | `biomes.ts` | `tests/unit/world`, D1, D2, P1 |
| Bare mid-slopes | `KEEPOUT_K` from .05 toward .067, the terrain's own K [I: T01 and §5.6], then far bands | `biomes.ts` | O1, D1. T01 has room: 5.8% against 15% at K = .05 [O: CPU] |
| Objects confused | Geometry, never `top` or `root` | `src/render/props/**` | T05's A20, A21 |
| Edges read white | Edge `L` | `src/render/props/**` | J5 |
| Far edge sparkle | Edge fade with distance | `src/render/props/**` | S1, S3 |
| Reflection too strong or weak | Mirror factor .42 | `src/render/props/**` | Must stay under the bloom threshold (§6.5) |
| CA, scan or grain obtrusive | §6.6 ranges | `src/render/fx/**` | Crops at 1:1; T06's A5 pin |
| Pulse reads backwards | One sign in `fract(v / 512. - phase)` | `terrain.frag.glsl` | `motion` strip; T03's pulse shots |
| Exposure too dark at night | Exposure floor .88 | `src/render/fx/postParams.ts` | W1, W2 at day-phase extremes; T06's A5 pin |

Day phase is sampled because exposure varies with it. `combos.ts` adds, for each palette, the zones nearest `dayPhase` 0 and .5, 12 extra `full` samples, for W1 and W2.

### M4. Photometry and palette

**Goal:** colour and glow correct in every pair and through every crossfade. **Work:** themes in order: global `L` and bloom balance against W3 to W5; **the crossfade path;** sky budget; then palette by palette.

**Crossfade.** T01 ships OKLCh (DI-05). Look at the hue sweeps: the largest change is .15 in a linear channel across m = .05, on Ultraviolet to Toxic's accent [arithmetic], so a sweep may look fast on the smoothstep's steep part. If a slot looks wrong, the rubric decides, with a Decision log entry, in `palettes.ts`.

**Sky budget.** §6.7 says sky up to .8. T11 changes those two horizons, Toxic Arcade's and Arctic Laser's, only if J4 or J5 objects.

**Result:** W1 to W5, C1, B1, X0, X1 green on all samples. J4 and J5 at the bar. **Proof:** A7, A9.

### M5. Density, silhouette and composition

**Goal:** "full of stuff", with variety, and the V and the sun intact. **Work:** per biome, using `pal-*`, `biome-*`, `worst-D1`. Likely themes: mid-slope bareness via `KEEPOUT_K`; biome signature strength; size hierarchy; Canyon reviewed by eye, since it is exempt from O1. Re-run T05's sheets after geometry changes. Then one sanity pass at seed 7, tile only, so tuning has not overfitted one seed. **Result:** O1, D1, D2 green. J1, J2, J3, J6, J7 at the bar. **Proof:** A7, A8, A10, A16.

### M6. Motion and transitions

**Goal:** the world holds together when moving, as far as proxies can tell. **Work:**

**Motion captures,** per biome at its first palette, computed in the page:

| Capture | Method | Output |
|---|---|---|
| Cruise | 48 frames at 1.3333 u (80 u/s, 60 Hz), neutral feel, `renderAt`, objects on | S1, S2 |
| Boost | 48 frames at 2.5 u (150 u/s) | S1 |
| Slit scans | 360 frames. Rows 560 (far objects), 612, 640 (floor). Column x = 700 | `slit-b<n>-r<row>.png`, 1920 x 360 |
| Heat maps | Mean `abs(dL)` and sparkle count per pixel, over 48 frames, amplified 8 times | `heat-b<n>.png` |
| Crop strips | 6 consecutive 320 x 180 crops of far band at 2x | `strip-b<n>.png` |

How to read them: in a slit scan, a stable object draws a smooth streak and shimmer draws speckle. In a heat map, activity should follow near lines, and a hot blob at range is a defect. These are proxies. They can show a defect. A clean result does not show smooth motion. That is why J10 stops at 2.

Budget: 360 frames with readback took 2.3 s [O], so the whole suite is seconds per biome.

**Transitions.** Sequences for three transitions, chosen by rule: the lowest X1 chroma, the largest luma difference between ends, and the opening's first. `renderAt` from `into` 72 through 103 at 1.3333 u: 1500 frames. Output:

- `trans-<a>-<b>.png`: 12 frames at 640 x 360, evenly spaced, in a 3 x 4 grid, so 1920 x 1440.
- `trans-<a>-<b>-curves.png`: mean chroma, lit saturation, luma and near-white against `into`. A dip is visible at a glance.
- Hero wipe viewed on any transition where `heroA` differs from `heroB`.

`transition.spec` also renders all 30 ordered pairs at m = .5 as `mid-sheet.png`, 6 x 5 at 320 x 180. On seed 1985 all 30 occur by zone 111 [O: rehearsal]. Biome crossfade is spawn-based and palette crossfade is continuous, so watch whether new objects arrive before the sky changes.

**Result:** S1 to S3, X2 green. J8, J10 at the bar. **Proof:** A9, A11.

### M7. First ten seconds and UI over the scene

**Goal:** the opening is chosen, not inherited. **Work:**

**Capture, on the production build, without harness:** serve `dist/` under `/art/`, start CDP `Page.startScreencast` before navigation through T10's `startScreencast`, keep frames with timestamps. T10 got 299 frames in 3 s this way, GL canvas included [O]. Playwright's `recordVideo` cannot run here, because it wants `ffmpeg-1013` [O: T10]. For F1 and F2, decode each through `createImageBitmap` onto a 64 x 36 canvas in a blank second page. Write `first10.png`: frames nearest t = 0, .25, .5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 10 s in a 3 x 4 grid at 640 x 360. Then click `launch` at 10 s, and write `launch.png`: 6 frames over the next 2 s.

**The opening today.** With T01's rehearsal code, seed 1985 gives [O]:

| Zone | Palette | Biome | Hero | `dayPhase` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Ultraviolet | Orbital Night | Banded sun | 0 |
| 1 | Miami Dusk | Boulevard | Ringed planet | .13 |
| 2 | Arctic Laser | Crystal Desert | Wire moon | .27 |
| 3 | Sunset Drive | Olympus | Eclipse | .40 |

So the first 80 seconds are a palette nobody has seen with objects, in a biome of scattered saucers and domes, at deepest night (exposure .88), and the look everyone has seen does not arrive until minute four. It may still look fine. Nobody has checked. T10's poster is Sunset Drive, hidden with no fade, so this opening also starts with a hard palette jump on the first frame. The first ring flythrough is at about 36 s in any case: chunk 48 from the sim's start at chunk 3, at cruise [arithmetic].

**Decision.** Round `r00` scores the opening as it stands. Then compare with candidates that open on Sunset Drive, Boulevard and the banded sun, all [O: rehearsal]:

| Seed | Zone 1 | Zone 2 | Zone 3 | All 36 pairs by zone |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 54 | Toxic, Neon City, wire | Ultraviolet, Orbital, ringed | Miami, Olympus, eclipse | 71 |
| 155 | Miami, Orbital, ringed | Ember, Neon City, eclipse | Arctic, Olympus, wire | 99 |
| 371 | Arctic, Orbital, eclipse | Ultraviolet, Desert, ringed | Miami, Neon City, wire | 123 |
| 575 | Toxic, Olympus, ringed | Miami, Canyon, eclipse | Ember, Orbital, wire | 142 |

Default direction: change the default seed unless 1985 scores at least as well on J9, because Sunset Drive with palms is the one image known to work [O]. Capture `first10` for each candidate via `?seed=`, plus a 4-zone sheet. Choose by J9 and J4, breaking ties on the earliest "all 36". Apply through `DEFAULT_SEED` under §8, which T10 reads. The operator can overrule at M10. The combos table regenerates from the seed automatically, and the T10 autopilot test re-runs at M9.

Every seed starts at `dayPhase` near 0, since it depends only on chunk. If deepest night hurts the opening on every candidate, the knob is the exposure floor, or reopening T01 to offset day phase. Log it.

**UI over the scene.** `play.spec`: real `launch` click, then `stepFrames` under `page.screenshot`, on each palette's first zone, in PLAY with the HUD, with the title in DEMO, in CRUISE. Then 40 s of boost with hits, sampling `full` metrics every 6 frames, and viewing the worst flash, glitch and boost frames against T06's reject list. A hit transient may reach 1.5% near-white [A], report-only for chroma while flash is above 0. Title and HUD legibility on all six palettes is viewed. A defect reopens T08.

**Result:** F1, F2 green. J9 at the bar. Opening decision logged. **Proof:** A12 to A15.

### M8. Budget and low tiers

**Goal:** the look fits, and degraded tiers still look intended. **Work:**

Run `budget.timing.spec`, alone on the machine (`Agents.md`). For each biome's tile zone, 120 frames in a tight loop through `renderAt`'s synced ms: all on; each layer hidden; `full`, `noCa`, `min`, `bare`, `off`; MSAA 2 and 0. Write `budget.json`, the marginal cost table. On the prototype: objects cost .2 ms p50 at MSAA 2 (1.1 against .9), and MSAA 4 to 0 saved .3 ms [O]. Its worst-case p95 is not trustworthy, as one run showed 9.4 ms immediately after another GPU and CPU job.

**What gets cut if P1 fails, in this order.** Apply one rung, re-measure, stop at the first rung where every biome passes. Rung 1 is first because §6.5 and §6.10 name the mirror as the first sacrifice, and only its reach goes.

| Rung | Cut | Owner path | Visual cost [I] | Check after |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Mirror reach:** mirrored draws stop at 600 u [A], not the archetype's `maxDist` | `src/render/props/**` | Least: distant reflections mostly fall outside the valley floor | J5, J7 |
| 2 | Additive volumes: searchlights from 8 to 4 on screen, beams 40% shorter | `src/render/props/**` | Low; also lowers haze | J3, W3 |
| 3 | Heaviest meshes [A]: ORB 480 to 288, PALM 300 to about 200, COLONNADE 200 to about 140 | `src/render/props/**` | Low at speed | T05's A20, A21; T05's A1 pin |
| 4 | Tier 3 MSAA 2 to 0 | `src/render/fx/quality.ts` | Unknown for moving objects. Far floor sparkle did not change: .924 against .928 [O], but the prototype's objects were static | S1, `crop-*`; pin |
| 5 | Tier 3 nebula 384 to 192 | same | Low, sky is soft | J4; pin |
| 6 | Tier 3 bloom levels 6 to 5 | same | Low | J5; pin |
| 7 | Quotas down 15% in the over-budget biome only, never below T01's floors | `biomes.ts` | Moderate, directly against J3 | D1, D2, J3 |
| 8 | Mirror off at tier 3 | `quality.ts` | High. **Needs the operator's OK** | Everything |

**Never cut:** grid, disc, palette or crossfade (§6.10); 1080p at tier 3, which defines the gate; the ship, gates, shards or hazards; density floors.

If rung 8 still fails, stop (U5). The 2.0 ms limit is itself [A], inferred from one GPU (§9.1). T11 does not relax it. Rungs 4 to 8 also change §6.10 and flip pins.

**Tiers.** `tiers.spec`, one page load per `?tier=N`: `tiers.png`, three biomes across tiers 4, 3, 2, 1; each must still look intended, with grid, disc and palette unchanged. W1, W2, W5 hold at tiers 4 to 1. `software.spec`: tier 0 at 640 x 360, P2, viewed. `aspect.png`: 21:9, 16:9, 4:3, 9:16 portrait; disc position and framing hold, HUD included, per §6.4's FOV cap. `reduced.png`: one tile per palette under reduced motion.

**Result:** P1, P2 green. **Proof:** A18 to A20.

### M9. Sweep, pin batch, independent review

**Goal:** the repository is whole again, and someone who did not do the tuning has looked. **Work:**

1. Run every suite. Classify each red: a real regression (fix in the window, or reopen), or **a pin:** a direct, expected result of a logged retune.
2. **Pin batch.** For each pin, T11 edits the expected-value line and writes one row: test id, file, old expectation, new expectation, the retune behind it. The reviewer agrees to the batch once. A red that is not a direct result of a logged retune is a defect and goes to its owner. Known so far: T01's A2 if `palettes.ts`'s table is copied in the test. T06's A5 if bloom intensity, CA, scan, grain or exposure changed. T06's A1 with T00's `fakeProfile` and T04's A12 if a ladder rung touched the tier table. T05's A1 if a mesh count or capacity changed. T09's stored shimmer baseline.
3. **Independent review.** A fresh agent, with no tuning context, gets only the images and this text:

   > You are reviewing an unfinished synthwave game from images alone. You have not seen its code, history or metrics. First open `plant-sheet.png`: some tiles have been deliberately damaged. List the tile numbers you think are damaged, then open `plant-key.json` and report your hits and false alarms. Then name the biome of each tile in `biome-blind.png` from these six: Boulevard, Neon City, Crystal Desert, Cyber Canyon, Orbital Night, Olympus. Name each object in the silhouette sheet. Then score the images with this rubric: [rubric table]. Give one line of evidence per score. Say what you could not judge from images. Do not soften.

   It reads from a copy of the round with `gates.txt`, `metrics.json`, `delta.txt` and the plan removed. Where it differs from the executor by 2 or more on a criterion, look at that evidence again and log the result. **This reduces anchoring. It is not independent confirmation of beauty** (section 2). If no subagent can be spawned here, record that, and tell the operator that their review is the only independent one.
4. **Close the record.** Diff the window against backup `r00`, confirm every changed file has ledger rows, and write the deviation register for the T00 owner or operator to apply. Expected entries: §5.1 default seed if it changes, plus any `KEEPOUT_K`, fog, tier, mirror, palette or limit changes.

**Result:** every suite green. **Proof:** A17, A21 to A24.

### M10. Operator review

**Goal:** a human decides. **Work:** run the pack (`pack.spec`, `TERRAIN_PACK=1`) to write `test-results/T11-art/final/index.html`: sheets, strips and curves, gate table with limits, the rubric results, known limits, the deviation register, and how to run it. The pack also lists every other plan's open Human checks. Clips: `clip-opening`, 30 s; `clip-transition`, 25 s on the worst pair; `clip-boost`, 10 s, through a raw RGBA pipe to `ffmpeg` if found. Rehearsed to `libx264` at CRF 14: 120 frames in 24.8 s [O]. Without `ffmpeg`, T10's `flipbook.html` plays the frames with no dependency. `yuv420p` softens 1 px neon, so **clips are an archive, not a judge. The live page is the review.**

**Ask the operator to do this, in this order:**

1. `npm run build`, then `$env:TERRAIN_PORT = '5111'; npm run preview`, and open `http://127.0.0.1:5111/` on their normal display, in a browser, full screen, sound on. **Before looking at any sheet.** Do not touch the input for 90 s.
2. Press a key or click. Play for about 3 minutes, including a boost and at least one zone change.
3. Then open the pack.

**Questions.** Record the answers verbatim in section 13.

| # | Question |
|---|---|
| Q1 | In the first ten seconds, what did you notice first, and did it make you want to keep watching? |
| Q2 | Is it unmistakably 80s synthwave? |
| Q3 | **Beautiful and stylish: accept, accept with the fixes you name, or reject?** |
| Q4 | Does it feel full of stuff, or are there empty or repetitive stretches? Where? |
| Q5 | Zone changes: did you see a grey-out, a pop or a hard change? Did it feel like travelling? |
| Q6 | Motion: any shimmer, strobing, discomfort or lack of speed, at cruise and on boost? |
| Q7 | On your display: is any colour wrong, any glow excessive, any dark area crushed or banded? |
| Q8 | Any palette or biome you would change or drop? See the 36-tile sheet |
| Q9 | Would you leave it running as a screensaver? |
| Q10 | Audio with picture: sync and mood. Answer T07's H1 to H13 |
| Q11 | HUD and title: readable, and in the way of anything? |
| Q12 | Any real mid-range PC or phone to try (§14 risk 1)? Tier reached, smoothness. Leave it running 10 minutes on that device: did it slow, and did the tier change? |
| Q13 | The opening seed choice, and any deviation-register entry you object to |
| Q14 | §14 risk 8: accept "no true pause", or add a still-frame option? Already open, restated here |
| Q15 | Switch tabs for 30 s, and come back: any jump or audio burst? |
| Q16 | Run the 2-hour soak, B22 |

**Acting on it.** Accept: finish. Accept with fixes: at most 2 feedback rounds [A] by the round protocol, then re-run acceptance and show the operator what changed. Reject with direction: treat as a new theme. Reject without direction, or no convergence after 2 rounds: `blocked`, with a written choice for the operator. **The operator's answer outranks every score, in either direction.**

**Result:** verdict recorded, then `done`. **Proof:** A25, A26.

### What would make T11 unable to finish

| # | Condition | State | Who resolves |
|---|---|---|---|
| U1 | T10 not done, or the harness lacks `renderAt`, `pixels`, `setPost` or `setLayerVisible` | `blocked` before M1 | T09, T10 |
| U2 | No aperiodic harness page, so the 36 real pairs cannot be reached. A forced-palette sheet would be false, because all tiles would show one biome | `blocked` at M1 | T10 |
| U3 | No real GPU: `gpu` project fails its rasterizer check. SwiftShader can only judge tier 0 | `blocked` | Operator |
| U4 | A fix needs a path outside the window: `src/ui`, `zones.ts`, `items.ts` | `blocked` until that plan is reopened. Other themes continue if independent | Operator or orchestrating reviewer reopens (`Agents.md`) |
| U5 | P1 fails after rung 8, or the operator refuses rung 8 | `blocked` | Operator: raise the [A] limit with real-device evidence, or accept lower density |
| U6 | Round cap reached with a 0 on any criterion or a red gate | `blocked`, with the best build, findings and choices | Operator |
| U7 | The reviewer fails strong plants twice | Viewing evidence void, `blocked` | Investigate the image path, then operator |
| U8 | **Operator cannot review, or does not converge after 2 rounds.** Everything else can be complete, and T11 still cannot say "done". §14 risk 2 stays open and `Agents.md` keeps T11 `blocked` until then | `blocked`, pack ready. The build is usable | Operator |
| U10 | Gates conflict: no value satisfies two gates in one pair, for example W1 and W2 on one palette | `blocked` on that pair. Changing a limit needs a Decision log entry and reviewer agreement | Reviewer, operator |
| U11 | The fix needs a non-numeric contract change, or moves a §6.1 invariant | Rejected by §8 | Operator, as a design change |
| U12 | A retune breaks R1: identity or warp suites turn red and cannot be fixed without dropping the look | Revert first. R1 wins | Operator if a look is lost |
| U13 | Neither haze metric fails DR1 on the real path | `blocked` | T00 owner, then operator |
| U14 | Neither D1 nor D2 fails DR4 on the real path | `blocked` | T00 owner, then operator |

## 6. Concrete steps

All from `C:\highsierralabs\terrain`, PowerShell 5.1. Env vars do not persist between tool calls, so set them in the same call. Never run `gpu-timing` with anything else on the GPU.

**Backup before each round, or drill:**

```powershell
$r = 'r00'; $d = "$env:TEMP\mv-T11-backup\$r"
$files = 'src\world\palettes.ts', 'src\world\biomes.ts', 'src\contracts\glsl\hero.glsl', 'src\feel\tuning.ts', 'src\sim\tuning.ts'
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force "$d\src" | Out-Null
Copy-Item -Recurse -Force src\render "$d\src\render"
foreach ($f in $files) { New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force (Split-Path "$d\$f") | Out-Null; Copy-Item -Force $f "$d\$f" }
Get-ChildItem -Recurse -File (@('src\render') + $files) | Get-FileHash -Algorithm SHA256 | Select-Object Hash, Path | Export-Csv "$d\manifest.csv" -NoTypeInformation
```

**Restore and verify:**

```powershell
$r = 'r00'; $d = "$env:TEMP\mv-T11-backup\$r"
$files = 'src\world\palettes.ts', 'src\world\biomes.ts', 'src\contracts\glsl\hero.glsl', 'src\feel\tuning.ts', 'src\sim\tuning.ts'
Remove-Item -Recurse -Force src\render; Copy-Item -Recurse -Force "$d\src\render" src\render
foreach ($f in $files) { Copy-Item -Force "$d\$f" $f }
$now = Get-ChildItem -Recurse -File (@('src\render') + $files) | Get-FileHash -Algorithm SHA256
Compare-Object ($now.Hash | Sort-Object) ((Import-Csv "$d\manifest.csv").Hash | Sort-Object)   # prints nothing when identical
```

**M0:**

```powershell
Select-String -Path plans\T*.md -Pattern '^status:' | ForEach-Object { "$($_.Filename)  $($_.Line)" }
npm run plans:check; npm run check; npm run build
$env:TERRAIN_PORT = '5111'; npm run test:smoke; npm run test:browser; npm run test:software; npm run test:forever; npm run test:perf
```

Each GPU suite runs alone through the machine's GPU lock, one command per call: `node <scratchpad>	ools\gpu-lock.mjs T11 "npm run test:browser -- --output=test-results/T11-M0-browser"`.

**M1:**

```powershell
npm run check:task -- T11
$env:TERRAIN_PORT = '5111'; $env:TERRAIN_ROUND = 'dev'; npx playwright test tests/art/metrics.spec.ts tests/art/capture.spec.ts --project=gpu --output=test-results/T11
```

**A full round, M2 onward:**

```powershell
npm run build
$env:TERRAIN_PORT = '5111'; $env:TERRAIN_ROUND = 'r01'; npm run test:art -- --output=test-results/T11
# Red is expected while tuning. Artifacts are written before assertions.
Get-Content test-results\T11-art\r01\gates.txt; Get-Content test-results\T11-art\r01\delta.txt
```

**One theme:**

```powershell
$env:TERRAIN_PORT = '5111'; $env:TERRAIN_ROUND = 'r02'; npx playwright test tests/art/transition.spec.ts --project=gpu --output=test-results/T11
npx vitest run tests/unit/world
```

**Planted sheet:**

```powershell
$env:TERRAIN_PORT = '5111'; $env:TERRAIN_ROUND = 'r00'; $env:TERRAIN_PLANT = '1'; npx playwright test tests/art/plant.spec.ts --project=gpu --output=test-results/T11
# Open plant-sheet.png with the Read tool. Write plant-answer.txt. Only then open plant-key.json.
# DI-101 retest: a fresh folder per run under test-results\T11-art\u7-retest\, for a fresh reviewer, never opened by the executor
$env:TERRAIN_PORT = '5111'; $env:TERRAIN_ROUND = 'r00'; $env:TERRAIN_PLANT_DIR = 'set-<UTC stamp>'; npx playwright test tests/art/plant.spec.ts --project=gpu --output=test-results/T11-plant
```

**T05's sheets, budget, software:**

```powershell
$env:TERRAIN_PORT = '5111'; npx playwright test tests/visual/props.spec.ts --project=gpu -g "sheet:" --output=test-results/T11-props
$env:TERRAIN_PORT = '5111'; $env:TERRAIN_ROUND = 'r05'; npx playwright test tests/art/budget.timing.spec.ts --project=gpu-timing --output=test-results/T11-timing
$env:TERRAIN_PORT = '5111'; $env:TERRAIN_ROUND = 'r05'; npx playwright test tests/art/software.spec.ts --project=software --output=test-results/T11-software
```

**Whole repo, then pack:**

```powershell
npm run check; npm run build
$env:TERRAIN_PORT = '5111'; npm run test:smoke; npm run test:browser; npm run test:software; npm run test:forever; npm run test:perf
$env:TERRAIN_PORT = '5111'; $env:TERRAIN_ROUND = 'final'; npm run test:art -- --output=test-results/T11
$env:TERRAIN_PORT = '5111'; $env:TERRAIN_ROUND = 'final'; $env:TERRAIN_PACK = '1'; npx playwright test tests/art/pack.spec.ts --project=gpu --output=test-results/T11
```

"Open" means the Read tool, and then writing what was seen. An image not opened does not count.

## 8. Idempotence and recovery

- **Safe to re-run:** every spec. Re-running a spec in the same round overwrites only that spec's files, and a new round never touches an older one. Playwright's `--output` is disposable, `test-results/T11-art/` is not. Do not delete `test-results` until the operator's verdict is recorded: the clean-state command in T00's plan would destroy the pack.
- **Determinism:** same round state gives the same frames, except that T00 reports a post-on frame can differ by 1/255 on one byte, cause unknown [O]. `delta.txt` ignores differences under 0.5 in any summary.
- **Clean state without git:** the restore block in section 6, to `r00` or any later round. `$env:TEMP\mv-T11-backup\` is outside the project, as `Agents.md` requires, but a temp folder can be purged, so the ledger also records every old value, and it is enough to rebuild by hand.
- **Interrupted drill:** a drill is never left in place. If a session dies mid-drill, the next session first runs the hash comparison against the latest backup. A mismatch means restore before anything else.
- **Interrupted round:** resume from the plan. Progress names the last completed round and the backup holding it.
- **Revert means revert:** partial reverts are not allowed. Restore the backup, re-apply what was kept, and note it.
- **Contract amendments** are not in the backup. Record them in section 12 and in the ledger.
