## 10. Surprises and discoveries

### Found in the `final5` stage, 2026-11-07

- **A defect the code predicted was already in the round's own clips, and strips at 320 x 180 hid it.** The DI-113 reviewer inferred from the code that a leg's top stands out of the ring's lower corner as it opens, and saw it in no frame; T11 had viewed the same passes at `final4` and seen nothing. A full-size diff of the deterministic transition clip, `final4` against `final5` at a 40-level threshold, found it at once: four patches of 16 to 161 pixels on the frame's bottom edge (frames 188, 332, 476, 548), each a pale block about 20 x 16 px at a member's lower corner at `final4`, a clean corner at `final5`; and the boost run's "worst shard" tile (frame 1164) showed the same block at 3x [O: `scratchpad T11/final5/clipdiff.cjs`, `frames/legs-*.png`, `b1164-corner.png`]. A frame-for-frame diff of a deterministic clip is a sharper instrument than reading strips, where it can be used.
- **An exact identity in IEEE arithmetic did not bring the pixels back.** DI-115 rewrote every step of the gate rule as the identity plus a term that is exactly 0 at `gateK` 1, yet the stills at `final5` equal `final4`'s per sample (W2 within 2.4e-6, W3 within 5.5e-6), not `final2`'s or `final3`'s (W3 9.40e-5 at p1b0i8, W2 2.70e-5 at p1b0i68, the same as `final4`); one full-size frame keeps a 14-pixel, 1-level cluster under the horizon that `final4` also had [O: `persample2.cjs`, `framediff.cjs`]. So the earlier inference that DI-113's move was rounding at k 1 (T11's and the reviewer's) is not supported; the cause is not traced.
- **The boost run did not move at all under DI-115:** floor black and near-white equal `final4`'s on all 556 samples, chroma within .021, where `final4` against the T00 owner's probe on one window moved floor black by up to 1 and chroma by up to 1.4 [O: `boostcmp.cjs`]. Run-to-run spread on this run is smaller than that probe suggested.

### Found in the `final4` stage, 2026-11-07

- **A rescale that is the identity in exact arithmetic still moved a stills metric.** Under DI-113 the stills W3 differs from `final3` by up to 9.4e-5 on 66 samples, about 14 times its earlier same-window spread, while every sheet gate stands at 22 u or more, where `gateK` is exactly 1 [O: `persample2.cjs`; I: the cause, rounding in `l.xy *= (GATE_MID + (r - GATE_MID) * t) / r`, not traced]. Harmless here (1/5,000 of the delta floor), but a same-window repeatability figure measured before a shader change does not carry over to after it.
- **The boost clip is not byte-repeatable between page loads,** though the boost run's per-frame sim is: two pack builds on one window put the same passed-gate member a few pixels apart (5.9% against 5.7% of the width at frame 167), with about 8% of pixels over 40 levels apart at frame 168 [O: `ffmpeg` difference of the two builds' frames]. The transition clip, a harness sequence, repeats within encoding noise (at most 23 levels). Frame-for-frame comparisons of `clip-boost` across rounds compare the same moment only approximately.
- **A colour-threshold width measure fails on a dark-faced member.** `barwidth.cjs` read DI-113's Toxic Arcade member as almost absent, because under DI-113 its face is dark green between two bright mint edges, and even `final3`'s 40% wall read as 4 to 28%; on Sunset Drive's pale peach members it agreed with viewing [O]. Widths there are read by eye.

### Found in the `final3` stage, 2026-11-07

- **Scaling a ring about the view axis moves members away only when they are away from the axis.** DI-110's `k` (up to 7) pushes an off-axis member out of the frame, but a member the axis passes through or near grows around the axis: in the boost run a gate face fills about nine tenths of frame 2190 (floor black 7 to 68), and in `clip-transition` a near-axis member covers 36 to 45% of the frame for three frames where it covered 11 to 24% before [O: viewed; `final` against `final3` differs only in `props.vert.glsl`]. A fix that works on the traced case (the ui-play slab) can fail on the neighbouring one; the boost run's per-frame gates found it within one run.
- **A gate leaving by scale flares pale.** `edgeScale *= k` widens the emissive edges with the ring, so in the cold-load opening the octagon turns pale peach-white with a glow halo for one or two frames before it leaves [O: frames 188, 189, 260, 332 of `clip-opening`; the cause is inferred from the shader].
- **A one-frame repeat in a screencast clip is not a sim repeat.** `clip-opening` frames 188 and 189 are the same picture: the 30 fps resample of the screencast duplicates frames, so frame counts in that clip are not durations [O].
- **The same window gives per-sample W2 differences up to 3.62e-5 between runs** (`a24` against `final2`), twelve times the 2.89e-6 this plan had cited as repeatability; one run, one sample (p3b5i68) [O: `scratchpad T11/final3/persample2.cjs`].
- **A sequence chosen by a rule over images can change when the image changes:** `transition.spec`'s "largest end luma difference" picked Ultraviolet > Arctic Laser at `final3` instead of Ultraviolet > Toxic Arcade, because an end frame's luma moved [I: a gate in an end frame].

### Found in the `final2` stage, 2026-11-07

- **`W4allow`'s worst frames are near-camera clutter, not a lifted black.** Frame 1499 (`final2/worst-floor0.png`) has a large flat peach gate octagon in the foreground and salmon hazard spikes over the floor band, so the band's 25th percentile rises; at `a24` frame 2133 had fence lines and a planet reflection there. And the pre-T11 window fails the same gate on more frames, by more (4 of 242, up to 17 against 13.43) [O: A/B, Progress]. The gate is sound as a wash-out test (its lift control is caught on 242 of 242), but on these frames what it measures is geometry next to the lens.
- **A seed change silently changed which transition the pack's clip shows.** `pack.spec` found "the lowest-chroma transition" by its zone number, measured by `transition.spec` at `ART_SEED`; at `DEFAULT_SEED` 155 the same zone number is a different palette change [O: zone 37 is Toxic Arcade > Sunset Drive at 1985; at 155 that palette change first occurs in zone 31]. Fixed by matching the palette pair. A test that reads another spec's output must check that both ran at the same seed.
- **One-pair-per-biome samples leave gaps that a seed change exposes.** At 155, motion's S1 far reads 7.342% on Toxic Arcade / Cyber Canyon, a pair the 1985 run never samples, and the same on `r00`; DI-109 found the same for A16b on the Boulevard opening. Both instruments sample one scene per biome, chosen by the seed.
- **The threaded-gate look is in the product's opening.** At seed 155, `first10.png` at 4.0 s has a pale full-height slab at the left edge, and `launch.png` at click + 1.2 s a flat peach gate frame around the whole picture as the camera passes through a gate [O: viewed]. DI-107's beam fade does not reach gates (A24 stage).
- **`dist/` was stale against DI-107's last write, and its bundle was not.** `dist/` dated 03:14:25Z, `props.frag.glsl` 03:22:28Z; the rebuild produced the same content-hashed `app-Wwc3OBsH.js` as batch5, now containing `nearB` [O: grep of the bundle]. So the 03:22:28Z write did not change the bytes the bundle saw [I].

### Found while executing M9 steps 3 and 4 and M10, 2026-11-07

- **A tool-using reviewer measured its way to the planted answers.** Four of its five hits came from statistics it computed on the sheets (bright-pixel saturation 106 against 181 to 212, a floor of (21, 20, 27), a channel maximum of about 150, 5,894 pixels at or above 253); by eye it found the missing layer only [O: the report, section 9]. Neither the README nor step 3's text forbade it. The same format and pool gave 3 of 7 to a reviewer that only looked.
- **The planted key contaminated the blind biome sheet.** The retest generator lists every unplanted tile's pair in the key, the blind sheet was cut from the same `tiles-seed1985/`, and step 3 opens the key first; 12 of 18 blind tiles were keyed frames [O: both keys compared].
- **The "near-camera beam" was a laser fence the ship had already threaded.** Its gap verticals were kept at full L at any distance, and fences between the camera and the ship plane (zf 2.4, 9.7, 10.0) drew full-height bars [O: `scratchpad T11/m9/near.mts` on three frames, each projecting to the bar's position]. About a third of samples hold a fence in front of the ship's tail, and 6 of 192 showed a visible bar.
- **T01's density floor for Crystal Desert sits at its margin.** Moving any share of the cacti from the 86 to 320 u band to the 50 to 84 u band (.6, .65, .7) empties the 16th screen column in `density.test.ts` [O: three vitest runs].
- **The floor beside the road is structurally empty near the camera.** Below about row 670 the frame's width lies inside the 44 u road corridor, where no scenery band reaches; only items and road furniture can stand there [arithmetic: horizontal half-tangent .9855 at 16:9, camera y 7.46].

### Found while executing M9 steps 1 to 3, 2026-11-06

- **Eight kept retunes flipped only one test in the whole repository.** No visual row of T03, T04, T05 or T06 asserts the sun column's shape, a palette value, the shard body, the ship's outline or Boulevard's quotas, so `test:browser` and `test:software` passed with M0's exact counts (531 and 52) [O: `scratchpad T11/m9/browser.txt`, `software.txt`]. The one red was T06's unit row that copies the flash slot. It also means those owners' suites would not have caught a bad retune of these values; T11's own gates and `delta.txt` did that work.
- **B11's flood control margin widened on the tuned build:** haze lift 20.73 against > 20, from 20.35 at M0 [O: `m9/forever.txt`]. Not caused deliberately; ledger rows 1 to 8 change what the flood control floods, and which one moved it was not separated.
- **A reviewer's images carried a measurement:** `first10.png`'s labels printed each frame's mean luma, F2's input, while step 3's text tells the reviewer it has not seen metrics. The label now carries time only [O: viewed `review-m9/first10-a.png`]. The first build of the folder also took in `ship-mask.png`, an O1 instrument image, by a loose name pattern; it was caught in the build's file list and left out.

### Found while executing M7 and M8, 2026-11-06

- **Shard and gate flashes lift the floor's blacks in real play, which no `renderAt` frame shows.** T02's feel adds flash .03 per shard and .12 per gate, and T06's Crt replaces dark surfaces' colour with the flash colour at luma .2 times flash, after tone mapping. In the r08 boost run, outside hits and SIGNAL_LOST, floor black by the frame's own `uFlash`: 0, median 4 (19 frames); .01 to .02, median 9; .02 to .04, median 11; .04 to .08, median 15, 11 of 11 over W4's 13 [O: `test-results/T11-art/r08/metrics-boost.json`]. Flash was above 0 in 167 of those 186 frames. Every stills gate runs under neutral feel, so W4 cannot see this; the boost run prints it. Not tuned (round cap, and it is T02's feel design with T06's formula); for the operator (Q7).
- **The first `play.spec` run counted designed effects as defects:** 116 of 361 "clean" frames failed W4 because SIGNAL_LOST holds glitch at .35 (its band swaps raise floor luma) and hit flashes last about 46 frames, not 12 [O: `m7/metrics-boost.json`, floor black 49 to 88 on those frames]. Classes by the Crt uniforms fixed it (Decision log).
- **The cold load is not DI-99's 5 s:** on the real page in a fresh browser, `data-mv-boot` reached `running` at 2247 to 2417 ms over six loads and the first lit frame followed within 45 ms [O: first10 at five seeds and once more at r09]. DI-99's figure is the harness page.
- **The poster's fallback caption shows for a frame or two** at about .23 s on two of six cold loads (seeds 54 and 155 at m7), then the title replaces it [O: `m7/first10-seed54.png`, `-seed155.png`]. A timing effect of T10's page.
- **Harness warps leave HUD state from the last place:** after warping through six palettes, CRUISE on Sunset Drive showed the sector banner "SECTOR 02 - ARCTIC LASER" [O: `r09/ui-cruise.png`]. Harness-only; real play cannot jump zones.
- **Boulevard's `off` row timed 3.8 ms p50, 6.9 p95** in `budget.timing`, the first direct-to-canvas render on the page, where the five later biomes read .7 to 1.0 [O: `m8/budget.txt`]. A first-use cost of the harness-only `off` path, not a §9.2 row.
- **In PLAY the ship takes hits while held at a lane wall,** and three hits end in SIGNAL_LOST, so a shot taken after a fixed number of frames can show a flash or the SIGNAL LOST card [O: `m7/ship-1.png`, Miami Dusk at +40]. `play.spec` now waits for 20 quiet frames at the target.

### Found while executing M4 to M6, 2026-11-06

- **The GPU lock runs its command through a shell.** A `-g "A6|hero-palette|..."` pattern inside the lock's command string became a pipe, and the run did nothing (exit 255, empty log) [O: r06's first launch; `gpu-lock.mjs` spawns with `shell: true`]. Patterns with `|` stay out of locked commands.
- **T04's A6 holds the wipe's speed, not only its ends.** Halving the wipe's soft edge (.6 to .3) raised the largest per-step change at the production rate from under 8 to 14 levels on eclipse to wire moon [O: A6 on r06's first form]. The edge width and the zone fade's rate are coupled: a sharper wipe needs a slower `uHero.z`, which T01's zones own.
- **T09's shimmer band sees the top of the sun column.** Softening the column's sides inside rows 615 to 650 raised B10's floor shimmer from 7.42 to 7.60, over its stored line of 7.58, while the same taper starting at rd.y -.06 left it at 7.419 [O: four B10 runs, r03]. B10 runs on zone 0 at period 1024, Ultraviolet, so it also sees r02's disc change; that alone read 7.4185, so the disc colour did not move it.

- **Page-load pose noise is large enough to fake a regression.** In r01's first run a fence post stood in front of the camera in one sample (C1 1.12 to 2.18) only because the DEMO ship was at x 2.036 when manual mode began, against .045 at r00 [O: `r01/worst-C1-1.png`, openHarness's print]. A fixed tick removes it: four loads read the same pose to three decimals.
- **The DEMO autopilot never flies at rest height after boot.** From tick 1200 to 14400 the ship's y stays between about 7.4 and 8.6 and x swings up to ±25 [O: the pose scan]. The pose closest to the plan's camera (x 0, y 6) is tick 2717, x -.079, y 7.46, so the camera sits about .5 u higher than at r00's near-boot pose.

### Found while executing DI-101 step 1, 2026-11-06, with blind test charts

- **The Read tool presented 2560 x 1440 with no resize notice,** and the reader still told 1 px checkers from flat grey (16 of 16) and read 8 px random text exactly [O: `scratchpad T11/u7/answer-2560x1440.txt`, scored after writing]. The planner's probe saw 3840 x 1620 arrive at 2000 x 844 with a notice (section 2). So if a size limit applies here, it lies above 2560 px wide in this tool version. Sheets stay at or under 1920 wide anyway.
- **A 3.37 MB PNG at 1920 x 1128 came through as well as an 88 KB one:** 36 of 36 pattern patches right, text exact from 6 px up apart from 7 px [O]. So byte size up to that point does not degrade the presentation either.
- **The reader sees a 2-level dark step when the step has an edge inside the patch:** reported at +2 levels 9 of 9 times, and never at +0 (0 of 9). It still missed lift .08, about 20 levels on the floor, on three whole tiles. So those misses are not a matter of resolution or level depth. A likely cause is that a planted tile carries no reference of its own, so its blacks can be judged only against other scenes [I]. The retest's 640 x 360 tiles do not supply a reference either, and DI-101 step 3 exists for that case.
- **Faint grey smears were seen in pure-black margins** beside the 1 px rows on two charts, where the file reads 0 [O: `score-chart.mts`, max channel 0 in that region]. This is a presentation or perception artifact. It changed no answer, but it shows the viewed image is not always exactly the file.

### Found while executing M0 to M3, 2026-11-06, on project code

- **The agent reviewer misses lifted blacks and strong desaturation in isolation.** Over two counted planted sheets: strong plants 7 of 10 found, weak 0 of 4, false alarms 4. Missed: lift .08 three times (Arctic Laser / Orbital Night, Toxic Arcade / Cyber Canyon, and one found only as uncertain), desat .4 once (Miami Dusk / Cyber Canyon). On a 320 x 180 tile those plants still raise floor black by about 20 code values or cut chroma by about 40% [O: `scratchpad T11/plantpath.mjs`], so the image path carries them. The planner's optimistic rehearsal (section 2) found lift .08 among hue-rotated copies of one scene; among 36 different real scenes it was not seen. The gates catch both: W4 fails 180 of 180 lift .08 frames, W2 or W5 141 of 180 desat .4 frames.
- **A tool notice can reveal a hidden key.** Regenerating `plant-key.json`, a file read earlier with `cat`, made the environment print its new contents in full before the sheet was opened. Attempt 2 was voided, and keys now go under a name never read (`TERRAIN_PLANT_TAG`).
- **Laser fences draw a translucent sheet across their whole span,** which changes every disc pixel behind it by more than 24 code values in `bare` [O: `test-results/T11-art/dev/diag-p2b5i59-mask.png`; props account for .879 of the disc, terrain for .038]. That, not scenery, is most of O1's per-sample failures, and it is the "red fences dominate" look note in numbers.
- **The sparkle metric counts true motion.** With all layers, S1 read 12 to 37% in every band, because near fences, gates and grid lines crossing pixels give large second differences. With props hidden, the far floor reads .07 to 4.97% at cruise and DR2's hard-step grid 8.94 to 12.31%, which separates; the mid floor does not (Boulevard clean 16.0% against DR2's 10.2%). The prototype's 2% and 9% described a floor with no objects.
- **Plain cruise steps the mean luma by 15.8 to 36.5 code values** as objects pass the camera, so a 1.0 absolute pop limit cannot hold; with props hidden plain cruise steps 1.46 to 1.65, and one zone's plain cruise read 4.26 in DR5's run (zone 4), probably a sky event, not identified.
- **B1 fails on real skies for reasons other than banding.** Terrain walls, beams and stars in the crop pulled it to .13; on the sky alone with stars rejected, Miami Dusk's steep blue channel still reads 1.19 to 1.22 in all 30 samples with no contour visible in a stretched crop (`scratchpad T11/b1-miami-stretch.png`), and the undithered control spans under 8 levels on 4 of 6 palettes.
- **DR8 is vacuous on `sheet36` at seed 1985:** none of the six Arctic Laser tiles has the banded sun, so the disc top never covers a large area. On `hero`, where `forceZone` shows Arctic Laser's banded sun, W1 fails at .963% with `#FFFFFF`.
- **Frames are not exactly reproducible between page loads.** `renderState` takes `camX` and `camY` from the sim's `prevX` and `prevY`, which the DEMO autopilot leaves wherever it was at `MV_READY`; nothing in `HarnessApi` sets them. Between two r00 runs W3's worst moved 18.52 to 18.66 and the third-worst W3 sample changed. `openHarness` now prints the ship's position; `delta.txt`'s noise floor absorbs most of it, but a margin under about 1% of a value is within this noise.
- **The opening on disk is Ultraviolet / Orbital Night,** not Ultraviolet / Crystal Desert as one look note says [O: Node `zoneAt` and the page's own `zoneAt` agree at every sample]. Sunset Drive / Boulevard first appears at zone 94, about two hours in.
- **Cold load of `dist/`, fresh browser, no harness:** navigation commits at 24 ms, `data-mv-boot` is `loading` at 56 ms and `running` at 2181 ms, the first lit live frame is at about 2.2 s, no black frame follows, and the brightest 64 x 36 frame is 103 code values. So the real page does not show DI-99's 5 s first harness frame. The yellow poster's disc becomes Ultraviolet's pale lilac disc at that moment, and the title covers the disc's upper half (`first10.png`).
- **The synthetic scene for A1n needed two changes before its controls behaved:** with sparse, fully saturated grid lines an image-space flood gave W4 9.41 (passing) and desat .4 gave W5 .710 (passing). Denser, slightly less saturated lines fixed both. On the real frames the same overlap is what made W5 report-only.
- **A drill in a partial run cannot test a cross-biome ratio:** DR4 on Orbital Night alone left D1 ratio's other medians empty (NaN). D1 and D2 failed anyway.

### From the 2026-11-05 planning probes

None of these ran against project code.

- **OKLab crossfades turn the world beige.** Straight OKLab lines through near-neutral between complementary neons. Sunset Drive to Toxic Arcade's grid at m = .5 is `#caa8a1`, saturation .20, from two ends of 1.00. Grid or accent saturation falls under .4 on 28 of 60 ordered slot-pairs at some m in .25, .5, .75 [arithmetic]. Rendered on T03's rehearsal terrain and sky through DevPost: mean chroma 27.1 at the midpoint, against 87 and 68.8 at the ends, and it fails the §6.7 gate of 55. Viewed: a grey-beige grid under a muddy sky. Toxic to Ember 39.2. OKLCh with shortest hue gives 56.3 and 55.8, and viewed they stay neon. For those pairs the grid sits under saturation .5 for 7 to 9 s of each 20 s crossfade [arithmetic], and a zone lasts 80 s. So this was going to cost about a tenth of play time on those pairs. No sibling plan has measured a palette midpoint: T01 tests only finite and clamped, and T10 will view one mid-fade frame. (closed by DI-05)
- **Near-white and chroma pass floods on the real scene as well as T06's card.** The flood frame had .007% near-white and chroma 72 to 76, higher than clean. Section 5 has the table. Viewed at gain 3 there are also contour rings in the bloom halo, which no metric catches.
- **Haze lift in the centre ellipse separates better than whole-frame:** 9.0 to 58.7, against 6.4 to 35, and threshold .1 at intensity .8 reads 21.1, where T06's whole-frame figure stayed near 12. (Named centre haze since S-53.)
- **The default seed opens on Ultraviolet, Orbital Night, deepest night.** Sunset Drive first arrives at zone 3, 4 minutes in. 12 seeds under 2500 open on Sunset Drive, Boulevard and the banded sun. Computed with T01's rehearsal `entry`, which T01's plan copies; it should match, but confirm at M1.
- **`forceZone` cannot produce the 36-tile sheet,** and neither can the default harness world. `overrideZone` leaves terrain and props on the chunk's real biome (T01), and at `periodChunks = 1024` only zones 0 to 10 exist. A sheet built naively either way would look complete and be false. The truth checks in `capture.spec` exist because of this.
- **How the reviewer sees images.** 1920 x 1080 arrived unscaled, while 3840 x 1620 arrived at 2000 x 844 with a notice, and its 1 px gratings aliased into moire that does not exist [O: test chart]. T06's section 5 says a 1080p image is downscaled when viewed. That did not happen here. It may differ by tool version, so sheets stay under 1920 wide anyway. Steps of one code level were not visible.
- **Blind rehearsal of the reviewer.** Answer written first, then key: `blind_answer.txt` before `blind_key.json`. 10 planted, 9 flagged (one only as uncertain), 1 missed (`desat.2`), 1 false alarm flagged as uncertain (tile 26). Type calls were sometimes wrong: `toward.15` called desaturated, `th5b2` called flood. Strong on outliers, and optimistic for the reasons in section 2.
- **The art prototype's objects never move.** `placeChunks` moves terrain chunks. So every temporal figure from it concerns the floor. §14 risk 4 says "without moving objects", and the slit scans confirm it: vertical lines.
- **MSAA hardly moved the temporal numbers on that floor.** MSAA 2 against 0: far floor mean diff 1.83 and 1.82, sparkle .924 and .928. Filtered shading already handles it. It does not show MSAA is useless for moving silhouettes, which is why it stays rung 4 and is checked by eye.
- **Slit scans are a usable way for an agent to look at motion:** filtered against naive grid were distinguishable by eye on the floor rows. Not yet shown on moving objects.
- **Playwright's own `ffmpeg-1011` is no fallback.** Its demuxers are `image2pipe` and `matroska`, decoders `mjpeg` and `libvpx`, with no `rawvideo`. The PATH `ffmpeg` here is a full 9.0.2 build, not a project dependency. Clips are optional for this reason, and the live page is the real review.
- **One timing outlier:** p95 9.4 ms with objects on, p50 1.1, right after an encode on the same machine. Cause not established. It is a reason to keep timing runs alone.

## 12. Contract change requests

CCR numbers are kept stable because T10's plan cites them. The text below is kept as a record.

**CCR-T11-1. Pin a tier from the harness.** State: **rejected: withdrawn by its author**, C-33. `?tier=N` for page-level tiers; `HarnessApi.setTier` for switches.

**CCR-T11-2. Read the page's own zone.** State: **rejected: withdrawn by its author**, C-34. `window.__mvE2e.host.ctx.world.zoneAt` under `?harness` gives the same answer with no contract change. If T10 drops that hook, `capture.spec` fails, and this request comes back with the text `zoneAt(chunk: number, local: number): ZoneState` on `HarnessApi`.

**CCR-T11-3. Default seed as a retunable constant.** State: **approved**, C-35, absorbed into architecture.md §8 on 2026-11-05; §8's wording wins. §5.1 fixes 1985, which opens on Ultraviolet at deepest night. T11 needs to be able to choose the opening. §8's exception covers numeric look constants, and this is one. `src/contracts/index.ts`, after `WorldOptions`:

```ts
/** §5.1. World seed when no ?seed= is given. It decides the opening, so T11 may retune it under §8. */
export const DEFAULT_SEED = 1985;
```

T00's `contracts-check` allowlist adds `DEFAULT_SEED`, beside `FOG_START` and `FOG_END`. T10's `params.ts` reads it.

**CCR-T11-4. Name the harness world's period.** State: **amended**, C-36, absorbed into architecture.md §8 on 2026-11-05; §8's wording wins. Comment only. §5.1 says harness mode passes `periodChunks = 1024`. §11.3's aperiodic warp and T11's 36 pairs both need period 0, and the contract does not say how. CCR-T10-1 states the same thing above `HarnessHost`, so a reconciler may keep either wording. `src/contracts/index.ts`, above `HarnessApi`:

```ts
/** Real page query: ?harness enables window.__mv. Under it, ?period=<n> sets WorldOptions.periodChunks, default 1024, 0 = aperiodic. ?seed=<int> overrides DEFAULT_SEED in any mode. */
```

If T10's URL table changes and nothing replaces it, U2.

**Numeric retunes during T11.** `FOG_START`, `FOG_END` and `DEFAULT_SEED` will be filed here with old value, new value, bound and evidence, for the T00 owner, with T03's A9 and T09's edge paint re-run.

**CCR-T11-5. `DEFAULT_SEED` 1985 to 155.** State: **approved** by the T00 owner, DI-102 (2026-11-07), applied with fakes on `FAKE_SEED` and the art suite on `ART_SEED`, both 1985. Filed 2026-11-06T22:06Z for the T00 owner, under §8's numeric exception. The diff, in `src/contracts/index.ts`, with the comment unchanged:

```ts
export const DEFAULT_SEED = 155;
```

- **Why:** M7's comparison (section 9, A14): 1985 opens on Ultraviolet / Orbital Night, a one-frame jump from the yellow poster disc to a pink disc at about 2.3 s, J4 1 on its opening zone; 155 opens on Sunset Drive / Boulevard / banded sun, continuous with T10's poster, then Miami Dusk, Ember and Arctic Laser, J4 3, 3, 2, 3, all 36 pairs by zone 99. Evidence: `test-results/T11-art/m7/first10*.png`, `opening-seed*.png`, `r09/first10-seed155.png`, `launch-seed155.png`.
- **Bound:** §8 states none beyond an integer; `createWorld` takes any safe integer. FOG is not involved, so T03's A9 and T09's edge paint are unaffected.
- **Checked at 155 before filing:** `first10` (A13) F1 2366 ms, F2 93.5, on the r09 window; the opening's six frames pass W1, W2, W4, C1; T10's autopilot measure (a read-only copy of `realWorld.test.ts`'s call) 279 gates, 100% threaded, longest still .075 s. **Not run at 155:** A7 `sheet36`, A9 `transition`, A10, A11, and every suite outside `tests/art`.
- **Ripple, for the owner's decision:** `src/testing/fakeWorld.ts:88` defaults every fake world to `DEFAULT_SEED`, so dev-page rows of T03 to T06 that build `createFakeWorld()` without a seed see a new world, and `tests/unit/core/fakeHost.test.ts:14` asserts `1985`. T09's B10 (shimmer, zone 0 at period 1024, stored baseline 7.4353) and B11 (flood control at tick 1200, whose palette is forced but whose terrain and props follow the chunk's real biome, margin 20.35 against 20) both run on the default seed. T10's `params.test.ts`, `D-001.test.ts` and `realWorld.test.ts` read the constant. T11's own round bases (r00p to r09) are all at 1985. If the owner prefers a smaller ripple, keeping fakes on a literal 1985 is a T00 change in `src/testing`, which T11 cannot make.

## 13. Outcomes and retrospective

**Where T11 stands: the agent stage is complete and the plan is `blocked` on U8, waiting for the operator's review (Q1 to Q16).** No report may call the build finished until the operator's verdict is recorded here (`Agents.md`, §14 risk 2). The operator's answers go below, verbatim, under "Operator's answers". **The pack is `test-results/T11-art/final5/index.html`** (round `final5`, 2026-11-07: the window `m10-final` plus the T00 owner's gate opening in `props.vert.glsl`, `material.ts` and `layer.ts`, DI-113 as corrected by DI-115; DI-110's gate rule replaced, DI-107 withdrawn). **Gates at `final5`: one red, `W4allow` on 2 of 242 boost frames (1734 and 2133, floor black 14 against 13.01 and 13.07), both red on the pre-T11 window `r00` too, so not T11's rounds' and not the gate rule's. Every other gated row passes, as at `final4`.** DI-115 moved no gate: the boost run's floor black and near-white are `final4`'s on all 556 samples, and the stills are `final4`'s within the spread between runs; on view it removed a small stepped block at a passed gate's lower corner (section 9, `final5` stage).

### Two seeds: what was measured where

- **The product seed, 155** (`DEFAULT_SEED`, DI-102): Sunset Drive / Boulevard / banded sun first, then Miami Dusk / Orbital Night, Ember / Neon City and Arctic Laser / Olympus. What you see when you run the game. In this pack: `first10.png` and `launch.png` (a cold load of `dist/`, A13), `opening-seed155.png` (the first four zones), the three clips, and motion at 155 as a report (round `final5-seed155`, not in the gate table). **T09's B10 was run at 155 as a report** (DI-112, in batch6, and again by the T00 owner in batch7 and batch8): the floor, props hidden, 6.294 every time, against its absolute limit 8 and above its liveness floor 1; its stored-baseline row there compares two scenes and is no verdict (A24 table).
- **The calibration seed, 1985** (`ART_SEED`): every other sheet and every gate in the pack, because the limits were set on its real frames at M2 and the art was tuned on it. A seed decides which zone comes first and where things stand, not how a palette or a biome is drawn. T09's harness suites (B10, B11, perf, DI-108) and T05's A16b (DI-109) also measure at 1985.
- **What that leaves:** nothing at 155 is gated except `first10`'s F1 and F2 and the opening sheet's six frames (B10's run there is a report). Two readings at 155 are over a 1985-calibrated limit, both on scenes that 1985 never sampled for that metric: T05's A16b on the Boulevard opening (props' blurred shimmer share .588 against .4, DI-109; not A/B'd against `r00` by anyone, so T11's share in it is not established), and S1 far floor, props hidden, on Toxic Arcade / Cyber Canyon (7.342% against 7%, motion at 155), which reads the same, 7.342%, on the pre-T11 window `r00` (A/B, section 9), so T11's rounds did not cause it.

The executor's tile scores at r06: J1 18, J4 25 of 36 at 2 or more, under the bar of 30; J2 34, J3 36, J5 30, J7 36. The M9 reviewer: J1 1, J3 1, J5 1, J7 1, J2, J4, J6, J8, J11 2. General feedback, verbatim:

> - Remove the support legs for the rings, the posts on the bottom left and right of the rings.
> - Most of the path is left and right movements by the player. Vary the rings height off of ground. The play should feel like more a swooping path.
> - The biggest issue is the player's vehicle. When flying around the camera appears to see through the vehicle at times. When it is close to the ground, panels on it dissappear as you move around. There appears to be a floating triangle directly below the vehicle.

**Verdict: accept with fixes** (Q3). The fixes are the three feedback items. The legs and the ship are T05's paths (`src/render/props/items.ts`, `ship.ts`, `glsl/props.vert.glsl`), which T11's window (`src/render/**`) also covers; the ring heights are T01's (`src/world/items.ts`, `lanes.ts`), outside the window. The T00 owner reopened T05 and T01 and made all three fixes as the operator's delegate (DI-118), outside T11's rounds, as with ledgers 13 to 16. T11's part is the feedback round on the result (round `op1`, base `final5`) and A26. **Q13** raises no objection to the seed and names no register entry; it does not mention the two `W4allow` frames (1734, 2133), so they stay a named choice and are not accepted by silence. **Q14** accepts "no true pause", closing §14 risk 8. **Q16:** at 13:02 Pacific, no soak process was running on this PC (no Playwright or soak process; the only listener on 5100 was the operator's preview, PID 35208). B22 stays open until its log exists. **Q10** is one line for T07's H1 to H13 together, not an item-by-item answer.

### Viewing sensitivity: what an agent's look can and cannot see (DI-101)

**Viewing sensitivity, recorded now for the operator pack (DI-101 step 3, 2026-11-06T18:34Z).** An agent reading images, at the validated format (3 x 3 tiles of 640 x 360, native size), found a missing object layer and a bloom flood, and found a 40% dimming once in two. It did not see a black lift of 8%, nor a 40% desaturation (0 of 3 across those two types), nor a 20% desaturation. **What the viewing in this plan cannot see:** lifted blacks, desaturation and dimming of a whole frame. Those are caught only by numbers: W4 for lifted blacks on every real frame, W2 for desaturation and dimming on 137 of 180 real frames. **On Miami Dusk and Ember, a 40% desaturation or dimming passes every gate** (43 of 180 frames), so there the only guard is `delta.txt`'s round-to-round comparison with `r00`, which catches a drift caused by tuning but not a defect that was already present at `r00`. Every rubric score in this plan that rests on colour grading (J4's "grey or muddy", J5's haze) is therefore an agent's reading that the planted test did not validate; the operator's display is the check (Q7).

**Viewing sensitivity, all three planted sheets at DI-101's format, measured (2026-11-07):**

| Reader | Sheet | Strong found | Weak found | False alarms | How |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Executor | r00, two 6 x 6 sheets of 320 x 180 (before DI-101) | 7 of 10 | 0 of 4 | 4 | by eye |
| Fresh reviewer 1 | `u7-retest/set-20261106T1821Z`, 3 x 3 of 640 x 360 | 3 of 7 (noprops, flood, one dim .6) | 0 of 1 | 0 | by eye |
| Fresh reviewer 2 (M9) | `review-m9/plant`, 3 x 3 of 640 x 360 | 5 of 5 (one of each strong type) | 0 of 1 (`glow`) | 0 | noprops by eye; desat .4, lift .08, dim .6 and flood by **pixel statistics it computed** (saturation, black level, channel maximum, clipping) |

**Reading:** by eye, across both fresh reviewers, a missing object layer was found 2 of 2 times and a bloom flood 1 of 2; lifted blacks 0 of 1, desaturation 0 of 2, dimming 1 of 2 (marked uncertain). A reviewer allowed to measure its images found every strong type once. **DI-101 step 3 therefore still governs this plan's viewing**: an agent's look counts for missing layers and floods; whole-frame grading counts only through W2, W4 and `delta.txt`, with the 43-frame hole on Miami Dusk and Ember stated above. The M9 reviewer's scores (section 9) were made by a reader that did measure, on one set of 6 plants. The operator may overrule at M10 (Q13), in either direction.

### What was not verified, and lessons

**Not verified by T11:** motion as a person sees it (every motion figure is a proxy); colour on any display but this pipeline's 8-bit output; the opening at seed 155 beyond `first10`, the opening sheet, the clips and motion as a report (A7, A9, A10 not run there; A11's motion at 155 is printed, not gated); A16b at the Boulevard opening against `r00` (DI-109 A/B'd DI-107 only); what the pale slab at 4.0 s of `final2/first10.png` is (DI-110 calls it the gate being threaded; T11 did not trace it); A8's per-pair O1 at seed 7 (one sample per pair only), and O1 at seed 7 under the fixed ship mask's rules; whether the first A24 reviewer accepts these answers (not asked again; the second review is a separate one, and it did not see DI-110 or `final3`); the boost run beyond six runs (`W4allow` 2, 3 and 3 of 242 before DI-110, 5 at `final3`, 2 at `final4`, 4 on `r00`); which gate records frames 693 and 2190 of the boost run show (the samples carry no chunk), and frame 693 itself (not stashed, so not viewed), including its rise from 11 to 15 against `final` under DI-113; frame 1152 of the opening's transition, where the all-layers step rose to 51.4 at `final3`, and frame 1432, where it reads 24.9 at `final4` (neither written as an image); the cause of the pale peach-white flare on a gate the autopilot threads in the cold-load opening (the gate flash, bloom or the members' lit face; not isolated, at `final3` or `final4`); the cause of W3's 9.4e-5 move under DI-113 (not traced; it survives DI-115's exact identity at k 1, so it is not the rounding both readers inferred, or this GPU's compiled shader does not keep the identity), and of the 14-pixel, 1-level cluster under the horizon in `worst-C1-1` (p2b2i23) shared by `final4` and `final5`; DI-115's shader text, which no reviewer has read (the DI-113 review saw DI-113's); the legs on a low pass close to a gate side in real play (the clips' camera passes are scripted, and the boost clip, not repeatable between loads, was judged by eye only; the T00 owner's probe is the only low-camera evidence); the transition clip's member widths at `final4` (read by eye: the width script's colour test failed on the dark-faced mint member); how a member the camera flies through reads at 60 Hz on a real display; how often a gate member crosses the camera's axis in a player's real play (two boost-run frames in 40 s and three clip passes of ten viewed, one scripted weave each); whether the gold gate frame at `ship-1`'s x = -40 tile was threaded; B10 at seed 155 beyond DI-112's one report run, whose seed is inferred, not printed; overlapping popups (DI-99); mobile risks (§14 risk 1: T05's `gl.flush()` every 12th props draw, T08's warm panels at opacity .001), since no phone is here; the clips' faithfulness to 1 px neon (yuv420p).

**Lessons.** A planted test measures the reader and its method together: two fresh reviewers at the same format gave 3 of 7 by eye and 5 of 5 with statistics, so the method has to be stated with the number. A blind sheet must not share frames with any key the reviewer opens first. A fixed measuring pose (r01) mattered more than any single retune: before it, round deltas followed page-load noise. Most "this looks wrong" findings here were geometry next to the camera (a fence the ship had passed, a post at the frame edge), which no stills gate looks for; reading the record from the world (`near.mts`) found the cause in minutes. And twelve ledger rows flipped one test in the whole repository (pin P1); `test:browser` and `test:software` kept M0's exact counts at M9 and again at M10, so other owners' suites would not have caught a bad retune of these values: T11's own gates and `delta.txt` did that work.

Known not verifiable by T11 regardless: beauty as anything but the operator's verdict; any GPU but this RTX and SwiftShader, so R6 stays a target (§14 risk 1); phones; displays other than the operator's; other browsers; whether thresholds tuned on seed 1985 generalise beyond the seed 7 sample; whether an agent reviewer's sensitivity on real sheets matches its sensitivity on the planted sheet, since plants are artificial; and hours-long viewing. A pass here says the frames are free of the defects these gates and reviewers can see.
